By demonstrating an effective method for disrupting face-swapping, the paper offers a practical solution to mitigate potential privacy risks and misinformation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Targeted Attack:** The paper effectively disrupts face-swapping models.
    *   **Visual Plausibility:** The method preserves visual quality by focusing on low-frequency perturbations, avoiding noticeable artifacts that could alert users to tampering.
    *   **Comprehensive Architecture:** The encoder-perturbation generator-decoder architecture is well-designed and automates the perturbation process.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments on CelebA-HQ and LFW datasets demonstrate consistent and significant improvements in defense success rates compared to existing methods.
    *   **Demonstrated Generalization:** The framework can function with a high performance on both the CelebA-HQ and LFW datasets, allowing for strong generalization.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Evaluation on Face Reenactment:** The paper acknowledges that the effectiveness against face reenactment manipulations isn't as thoroughly tested, and states this is an area for future work. This omission somewhat limits the generalizability of the defense mechanism.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** While the study attempts to find optimal parameter values through Ablation Studies, the success of the approach still relies on correctly fine-tuning for the environment. Small perturbations will not affect the generative model at all.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Adaptive Attacks:** The paper doesn't fully explore the potential for adversaries to develop adaptive attacks specifically designed to circumvent the proposed defense mechanism. While the current approach shows promise, its long-term effectiveness needs to be assessed against more sophisticated attacks.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the development of more robust and proactive deepfake defense systems. By demonstrating the effectiveness of targeting generative processes with subtle perturbations, it opens up new avenues for research in this critical area. Future research may build upon this work to create more adaptive and generalizable defense mechanisms that can withstand evolving deepfake techniques.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field of deepfake defense by introducing a novel approach that targets the generative process of face-swapping models. The use of low-frequency perturbations and the DWT-based architecture are innovative and lead to significant improvements in defense success rates while maintaining visual plausibility. The experimental results are compelling and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.

However, the limitations discussed above, particularly the lack of extensive evaluation against adaptive attacks and face reenactment, prevent it from achieving a higher score. While the paper demonstrates a strong proof-of-concept, further research is needed to address these weaknesses and establish the long-term viability of the approach. Nonetheless, the paper represents a significant step forward in active deepfake defense and has the potential to stimulate further innovation in this critical area.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Physics Informed Generative Models for Magnetic Field Images
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20612v1)
- **Authors**: Aye Phyu Phyu Aung, Lucas Lum, Zhansen Shi, Wen Qiu, Bernice Zee, JM Chin, Yeow Kheng Lim, J. Senthilnath
- **Abstract**: In semiconductor manufacturing, defect detection and localization are critical to ensuring product quality and yield. While X-ray imaging is a reliable non-destructive testing method, it is memory-intensive and time-consuming for large-scale scanning, Magnetic Field Imaging (MFI) offers a more efficient means to localize regions of interest (ROI) for targeted X-ray scanning. However, the limited availability of MFI datasets due to proprietary concerns presents a significant bottleneck for training machine learning (ML) models using MFI. To address this challenge, we consider an ML-driven approach leveraging diffusion models with two physical constraints. We propose Physics Informed Generative Models for Magnetic Field Images (PI-GenMFI) to generate synthetic MFI samples by integrating specific physical information. We generate MFI images for the most common defect types: power shorts. These synthetic images will serve as training data for ML algorithms designed to localize defect areas efficiently. To evaluate generated MFIs, we compare our model to SOTA generative models from both variational autoencoder (VAE) and diffusion methods. We present a domain expert evaluation to assess the generated samples. In addition, we present qualitative and quantitative evaluation using various metrics used for image generation and signal processing, showing promising results to optimize the defect localization process.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the scarcity of Magnetic Field Imaging (MFI) datasets for training machine learning (ML) models in semiconductor defect localization. To overcome this limitation, the authors propose a novel approach called Physics Informed Generative Models for Magnetic Field Images (PI-GenMFI). PI-GenMFI uses diffusion models and integrates electromagnetic physical constraints (Gauss's Law for Magnetism and Ampere's Law via a boundary condition) as regularization losses. The method generates synthetic MFI images of power shorts to be used as training data. The authors evaluate their approach against several baselines (VAE, VAE+Physics, and standard DDPM) using both quantitative metrics (PSNR, SSIM, FID, Fourier Score, Divergence Score) and qualitative evaluations by domain experts. The results show that PI-GenMFI, particularly the version with image enhancement, generates more realistic MFI images that align better with electromagnetic principles compared to the baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the application of physics-informed generative models, specifically diffusion models, to the domain of magnetic field image synthesis for semiconductor defect localization. While diffusion models and physics-informed machine learning are established techniques, their combination and application to this specific problem are relatively new. The formulation of physics-based constraints derived from Maxwell's equations for regularizing MFI generation is a tangible contribution.  However, the physics constraints used (Gauss's Law and a simplistic boundary condition based on Ampere's Law) are fundamental and not particularly sophisticated. Further exploration of more advanced electromagnetic principles might significantly increase the novelty. The idea of using synthetic MFI data to train ML models for defect localization is itself not entirely new, but the use of physics-informed generative models is a significant step forward.

**Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to alleviate the data bottleneck in MFI-based failure analysis. If the generated data is of sufficient quality, it can enable the development and deployment of ML models for defect localization, which could significantly speed up the failure analysis process in semiconductor manufacturing. The experimental results, particularly the domain expert evaluations, suggest that the generated data is reasonably useful, although some issues like oversaturation are identified. A major limitation is the small size of the real MFI datasets used for training. The generalizability of the findings needs to be validated across a larger range of datasets of different defects as well.  The quantitative metrics are promising but require careful interpretation considering the non-natural image domain. The overall contribution offers a practical step towards building more robust ML based fault localization.

**Strengths:**

*   Addresses a critical real-world problem: Data scarcity in MFI-based defect analysis.
*   Combines diffusion models with physics-based constraints, resulting in improved image generation.
*   Presents a comprehensive evaluation methodology, including both quantitative metrics and domain expert feedback.
*   The image enhancement techniques demonstrate a means for improving the MFI images

**Weaknesses:**

*   The physics constraints used are relatively simple and could be further developed.
*   The dataset used for training is very small.
*   FID may not be the ideal metric for evaluating MFI images, which deviate significantly from natural images.
*   The paper does not demonstrate training a defect localization model with generated data, only the generation of MFI images. Showing the usefulness of the generated images in training a localization model would significantly strengthen the paper.
*   The analysis of why Expert 2 found enhanced images to be unrealistic could be more insightful.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of semiconductor failure analysis by demonstrating the feasibility of using synthetic data generated by physics-informed generative models. This could lead to the development of new and more efficient defect localization techniques. It also encourages further research into the integration of physical constraints into generative models for scientific and engineering applications.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid contribution to addressing a practical problem. The combination of diffusion models and physics-informed constraints is novel in this specific application domain. While the physics constraints are basic and the dataset size is small, the results are encouraging and suggest that this approach has significant potential. The main reason for a lower score is the absence of a concrete demonstration on how the generated images can be used in training an AI model for defect localisation. Furthermore, the quantitative metrics should have been given more reasoning to why those metrics were selected. The paper has the potential to stimulate further research in this area, but its current impact is limited by its scope. Further advancements with more complex physics and larger datasets could push the score higher.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Schema-Guided Response Generation using Multi-Frame Dialogue State for Motivational Interviewing Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20635v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Zeng, Yukiko I. Nakano
- **Abstract**: The primary goal of Motivational Interviewing (MI) is to help clients build their own motivation for behavioral change. To support this in dialogue systems, it is essential to guide large language models (LLMs) to generate counselor responses aligned with MI principles. By employing a schema-guided approach, this study proposes a method for updating multi-frame dialogue states and a strategy decision mechanism that dynamically determines the response focus in a manner grounded in MI principles. The proposed method was implemented in a dialogue system and evaluated through a user study. Results showed that the proposed system successfully generated MI-favorable responses and effectively encouraged the user's (client's) deliberation by asking eliciting questions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, with a focus on rigor and justification:

**Summary**

The paper proposes a schema-guided approach to response generation for Motivational Interviewing (MI) dialogue systems.  It aims to improve response generation by enhancing dialogue state representation and dialogue strategies. It employs a multi-frame dialogue state, where each frame represents a different topic, and introduces a mechanism for producing frame-based dialogue strategies that guide Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate MI-aligned responses. The paper evaluates its system through a user study, comparing it against two prompt-based baselines: MI Few-shot and Principle-based Prompt Guide. The results suggest that the proposed system effectively elicits user thoughts, encourages reflection, and reduces MI-non-adherent behaviors.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of a multi-frame dialogue state representation with a schema-guided approach for MI response generation.  While schema-guided dialogue and prompt-based MI chatbots exist, the combination is a worthwhile incremental step.  Specifically, using multiple frames to track various, related topics in a single conversation provides a more complex and realistic approach to MI compared to previous work using fewer, more discrete representations. The method for generating dialogue strategies using LLMs trained on a corpus of existing MI knowledge has a degree of novelty; however, the core methods are rooted in existing schema-guided dialogue and LLM prompting techniques. The application to MI is not entirely novel, but the way they structured the information representation and strategy selection does add some value.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is moderate.  MI chatbots hold the potential to broaden access to valuable counseling interventions; the findings of a system that improves upon a more realistic depiction of a client state is therefore valuable. The results, suggesting the system effectively promotes change talk and reduces undesirable counselor behavior, support its potential effectiveness. That being said, the overall benefit is limited, as the MI domain is already being served in the literature and deployed in the real world.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Multi-Frame Dialogue State:** The use of a multi-frame dialogue state allows the system to manage multiple related topics within the conversation, mirroring the complexity of real-world MI sessions.
    *   **MI-Aligned Strategies:** By generating dialogue strategies based on MI knowledge, the system aligns its responses with established MI principles.
    *   **User Study:** The user study provides empirical evidence for the system's effectiveness in eliciting user thoughts and reducing MI-non-adherent behaviors.
    *   **Comparisons:** Comparisons with competitive prompt engineering approaches offer an indication that the multi-frame approach has value.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation focuses solely on dietary concerns, limiting the generalizability of the findings to other MI domains. A small data scale limits confidence in the findings.

    *   **LLM Dependency:** The system's reliance on LLMs raises concerns about the potential for biases, hallucinations, and generation of inappropriate content. These risks are mentioned in the ethics statement but need to be carefully addressed to ensure the system is safe and reliable.

    *   **Lack of validation from MI experts:** Even though the authors employed MITI in response analysis, a more convincing proof of the system's ability to conduct adequate MI could be validation from MI experts in addition to crowd-sourced responses from the users.
    *   **Subjective vs. Objective Improvement** Despite eliciting more contemplation in users, participants may not perceive the system as actively helping them with their journey due to less empathy.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in MI dialogue systems by demonstrating the effectiveness of schema-guided approaches with multi-frame dialogue states. It could also inspire the development of more sophisticated methods for aligning chatbot responses with MI principles.

*   **Score Justification:** I am assigning the paper a score of **6**. This score reflects the following factors:

    *   The novelty is incremental, building on existing schema-guided dialogue and prompt engineering techniques.
    *   The significance is moderate, addressing an important area (MI chatbots) but within a field that's already been explored by researchers in both academic and industrial spheres.
    *   The system has some strengths in design and evaluation, but the limitations in scope and potential reliance on imperfect LLMs reduce its overall impact.
    *   The results support the potential effectiveness of the proposed approach, encouraging the authors to validate the system and further explore more sophisticated dialogue strategies.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### GDS Agent: A Graph Algorithmic Reasoning Agent
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20637v1)
- **Authors**: Borun Shi, Ioannis Panagiotas
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown remarkable multimodal information processing and reasoning ability. When equipped with tools through function calling and enhanced with retrieval-augmented techniques, compound LLM-based systems can access closed data sources and answer questions about them. However, they still struggle to process and reason over large-scale graph-structure data. We introduce the GDS (Graph Data Science) agent in this technical report. The GDS agent introduces a comprehensive set of graph algorithms as tools, together with preprocessing (retrieval) and postprocessing of algorithm results, in a model context protocol (MCP) server. The server can be used with any modern LLM out-of-the-box. GDS agent allows users to ask any question that implicitly and intrinsically requires graph algorithmic reasoning about their data, and quickly obtain accurate and grounded answers. We also introduce a new benchmark that evaluates intermediate tool calls as well as final responses. The results indicate that GDS agent is able to solve a wide spectrum of graph tasks. We also provide detailed case studies for more open-ended tasks and study scenarios where the agent struggles. Finally, we discuss the remaining challenges and the future roadmap.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the GDS Agent paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the GDS (Graph Data Science) Agent, a system that integrates graph algorithms as tools within a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server, enabling Large Language Models (LLMs) to perform reasoning and analysis on graph-structured data. The GDS Agent enhances LLMs with a comprehensive set of graph algorithms, retrieval capabilities, and post-processing functionalities. It allows users to pose questions requiring graph algorithmic reasoning and receive accurate, grounded answers.  The paper also introduces a new benchmark for evaluating intermediate tool calls and final responses and provides case studies demonstrating the agent's capabilities and limitations. The results indicate the GDS agent can solve a range of graph tasks but struggles with issues such as large outputs and reasoning errors with specific graph analyses (like network capacity).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of graph algorithms as tools for LLMs within a standardized context protocol is a novel approach. While retrieval-augmented techniques and functional calling have been explored, the specific application to graph data science and the creation of a GDS-specialized agent are significant. The benchmark dataset and the analysis of tool call performance provide a valuable contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of LLMs: their inability to effectively process and reason about large-scale graph data. By providing LLMs with access to graph algorithms, the GDS Agent unlocks new possibilities for analyzing knowledge graphs and other graph-structured datasets. This has implications for various domains, including social network analysis, transportation planning, and fraud detection. The work potentially lowers the barrier to entry for users without deep graph analytics expertise to leverage these powerful tools.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of LLMs struggling with graph data and proposes a well-defined solution.
    *   **Comprehensive Integration:** The GDS Agent incorporates a wide range of graph algorithms and integrates them into a practical system with retrieval and post-processing capabilities.
    *   **Evaluation Benchmark:** The introduction of a new benchmark for evaluating graph reasoning agents is a valuable contribution to the field.
    *   **Detailed Case Studies:** The case studies provide insights into the agent's strengths and weaknesses, highlighting areas for future improvement.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reasoning Errors:** The identified reasoning errors when the agent attempts tasks without sufficient or proper tools, and the fact that it sometimes hallucinated an answer, need further investigation.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** Although the described architecture should work with a variety of LLMs, it would be interesting to see experiments conducted using a more diverse set of client models (e.g. open-source models).
    *   **Benchmark Scope:** While the new benchmark is an important step, its real-world applicability and the variety of graph reasoning tasks it covers could be further expanded. A more diverse set of graph datasets could also be incorporated.
    *   **Scalability:** While the paper showcases the ability to reason on large-scale graphs, it does not go into detail regarding the performance metrics when scaling up the graphs or the number of nodes. This is an important point to study.
*   **Potential Influence:** The GDS Agent has the potential to significantly influence the development of graph-based AI systems. By providing LLMs with access to graph algorithms, it can enable new applications and insights. The benchmark dataset and the evaluation methodology can also serve as a foundation for future research in this area.

*Score: 7*

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field by bridging the gap between LLMs and graph data science. The GDS Agent addresses a critical limitation of LLMs and offers a practical solution for analyzing graph-structured data. The benchmark dataset and the evaluation methodology provide valuable tools for future research. However, the agent exhibits some weaknesses, including reasoning errors and limitations in handling large outputs, which somewhat temper the score. Future research could focus on addressing these weaknesses, expanding the benchmark scope, and exploring the agent's potential in various domains. The overall influence of the paper on the field is expected to be substantial, but the identified limitations prevent it from receiving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### CraftGraffiti: Exploring Human Identity with Custom Graffiti Art via Facial-Preserving Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20640v1)
- **Authors**: Ayan Banerjee, Fernando Vilariño, Josep Lladós
- **Abstract**: Preserving facial identity under extreme stylistic transformation remains a major challenge in generative art. In graffiti, a high-contrast, abstract medium, subtle distortions to the eyes, nose, or mouth can erase the subject's recognizability, undermining both personal and cultural authenticity. We present CraftGraffiti, an end-to-end text-guided graffiti generation framework designed with facial feature preservation as a primary objective. Given an input image and a style and pose descriptive prompt, CraftGraffiti first applies graffiti style transfer via LoRA-fine-tuned pretrained diffusion transformer, then enforces identity fidelity through a face-consistent self-attention mechanism that augments attention layers with explicit identity embeddings. Pose customization is achieved without keypoints, using CLIP-guided prompt extension to enable dynamic re-posing while retaining facial coherence. We formally justify and empirically validate the "style-first, identity-after" paradigm, showing it reduces attribute drift compared to the reverse order. Quantitative results demonstrate competitive facial feature consistency and state-of-the-art aesthetic and human preference scores, while qualitative analyses and a live deployment at the Cruilla Festival highlight the system's real-world creative impact. CraftGraffiti advances the goal of identity-respectful AI-assisted artistry, offering a principled approach for blending stylistic freedom with recognizability in creative AI applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CraftGraffiti: Exploring Human Identity with Custom Graffiti Art via Facial-Preserving Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CraftGraffiti, a novel end-to-end text-guided framework for generating custom graffiti art while preserving the facial identity of the input person. The framework combines a LoRA-fine-tuned diffusion transformer for graffiti style transfer, a face-consistent self-attention mechanism for identity preservation, and CLIP-guided prompt extension for pose customization (without keypoints). The paper formally justifies and empirically validates a "style-first, identity-after" approach and shows it effectively reduces attribute drift in stylized output. The system is deployed at a music festival to collect user feedback on aesthetics, identity, and usability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its holistic approach to combining style transfer, identity preservation, and pose control within a diffusion model framework, specifically tailored for the graffiti art style. The face-consistent self-attention mechanism is a novel contribution. While each component (diffusion models, LoRA, CLIP guidance) is established, their specific integration for this task, along with the "style-first, identity-after" approach, adds a meaningful incremental value. However, the improvements in FFC is small.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant challenge in generative AI – preserving personal identity under extreme stylistic transformations.  This is important not just for artistic expression but also for fairness and representation, mitigating potential biases that arise when generative models distort or homogenize human features. The live deployment at a music festival demonstrates the real-world applicability of the system. The qualitative results showcase impressive preservation of facial features. The claim that this balance between stylization and identity is crucial for cases where cultural authenticity and personal identity are interwoven is important. The exploration of an artistic installation as a "living lab" is a relevant consideration in the context of discussing societal concerns with generative AI.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper provides a complete system with implementation details.
    *   **Strong Qualitative Results:** The visual examples are compelling and demonstrate the system's effectiveness in preserving facial identity.
    *   **Justified Design Choices:** The paper motivates the "style-first, identity-after" paradigm with a formal argument and empirical validation.
    *   **Real-World Deployment:** The Cruïlla festival deployment provides valuable insights into user perception and interaction.
    *   **Discussed Limitations:** It discusses the system's real-time generation complexity.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While the combination is novel, the underlying components are based on well-established techniques.
    *   **FFC scores:** While aesthetically CraftGraffiti outperforms competitors, the quantitative FFC metrics were not substantially better than that of its competition.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** FFC is not perfect and only consider global features. Local features (i.e. facial features are often ignored in FFC scores.
    *   **Societal impact:** It is important to acknowledge that the findings from deployment in Barcelona may not generalize to all socio-cultural settings. Also, more detail about user demographics and data collection process could improve the user evaluation findings.
    *   **Limited scope:** It doesn't tackle all societal concerns (age, body size, etc.), but it does establish identity as a key theme.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper provides insights into blending artistic control with identity preservation in generative models. The face-consistent self-attention mechanism could be adopted in other image editing applications where maintaining fine-grained control over specific regions is important. The Cruïlla festival is a good case study for deploying these kinds of technologies for public art demonstrations and could encourage other researchers to conduct real-world user studies.

* **Score:** 7.5

* **Rationale:** The paper makes a valuable contribution by combining existing techniques in a novel way to address the specific challenge of preserving human identity in graffiti art generation. The face-consistent self-attention mechanism is a novel component that addresses identity preservation. The implementation and demonstration are thorough. While the individual components are not groundbreaking, their combination is significant. The real-world user study enhances the value of the paper. The impact and influence are likely to be solid but not paradigm-shifting.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### VarDiU: A Variational Diffusive Upper Bound for One-Step Diffusion Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20646v1)
- **Authors**: Leyang Wang, Mingtian Zhang, Zijing Ou, David Barber
- **Abstract**: Recently, diffusion distillation methods have compressed thousand-step teacher diffusion models into one-step student generators while preserving sample quality. Most existing approaches train the student model using a diffusive divergence whose gradient is approximated via the student's score function, learned through denoising score matching (DSM). Since DSM training is imperfect, the resulting gradient estimate is inevitably biased, leading to sub-optimal performance. In this paper, we propose VarDiU (pronounced /va:rdju:/), a Variational Diffusive Upper Bound that admits an unbiased gradient estimator and can be directly applied to diffusion distillation. Using this objective, we compare our method with Diff-Instruct and demonstrate that it achieves higher generation quality and enables a more efficient and stable training procedure for one-step diffusion distillation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "VarDiU," a Variational Diffusive Upper Bound Distillation method designed to improve one-step diffusion distillation. The core idea is to train a one-step generator (student model) by minimizing a variational upper bound of the diffusive divergence between the student and a pre-trained teacher model. Unlike existing methods (like Diff-Instruct) that approximate the divergence gradient using the student's learned score function (obtained through denoising score matching, DSM), VarDiU provides an unbiased gradient estimator. The authors demonstrate that this unbiased estimation leads to more stable and efficient training and higher generation quality compared to approaches relying on biased DSM gradients. They conduct experiments on a 2D mixture of Gaussians dataset, showing VarDiU's superior performance in various settings (true score, training data, and learned score access).

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the variational upper bound formulation for the diffusive divergence in the context of one-step diffusion distillation.  Existing distillation methods relying on DSM inherently introduce bias through the imperfect learning of the score function. VarDiU addresses this by providing an unbiased gradient estimator for the distillation objective. The extension of the reverse KL upper bound to the diffusion setting and, further eliminating the joint entropy estimation is also interesting, but not as impactful as the unbiased gradient estimator itself.

* **Significance:** The significance of the paper is substantial.  One-step diffusion distillation is a promising direction for accelerating generative modeling, but stability and training efficiency are crucial challenges. VarDiU offers a principled way to address these challenges by avoiding biased gradient estimates.  If the empirical improvements demonstrated on a toy problem translate to larger, more complex datasets (like images), this approach could significantly impact the practical adoption of one-step diffusion models. The theoretical connection to information maximization also strengthens the paper's contribution.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Principled approach:** The variational upper bound provides a theoretically sound basis for distillation.
    * **Unbiased gradient estimation:** Addressing the inherent bias of DSM-based gradients is a key contribution.
    * **Empirical validation:** The experiments demonstrate improved stability, efficiency, and sample quality on a toy problem.
    * **Strong ablation studies:** Comparing VarDiU to Diff-Instruct under varying configurations of the training setting.
    * **Clear and concise explanation:** The paper is well-written and explains the method and its advantages clearly.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited experimental scope:** The experiments are confined to a relatively simple 2D mixture of Gaussians. The performance on real-world datasets (e.g., images) is not explored. The generalization ability of the approach to more complex data distributions and generative tasks has not been established.
    * **Dependence on teacher model's score:** VarDiU still leverages the teacher model's score function, making it dependent on the quality of pre-trained models.
    * **Computational Overhead:** While the paper shows the reduction in bias does improve the speed in finding a better gradient, it doesn't specifically mention if the cost of calculating the gradient is itself more expensive, for example, the use of Neural Spline Flow which is known to require more memory.

* **Potential Influence:**
    * VarDiU has the potential to become a standard technique for one-step diffusion distillation, especially if the benefits are confirmed on larger datasets.
    * The variational approach could inspire further research into alternative objective functions and training methods for diffusion models.
    * The analysis of biased gradients and their impact on training could lead to improvements in DSM and other score-based learning techniques.

**Justification for the Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7** to this paper.

The core idea of the unbiased gradient estimate through a variational bound and the overall method of VarDiU is sound and well-motivated, and the method does address an important issue regarding the bias during DSM score-based distillation. The empirical validation demonstrates clear improvements over existing methods on the simple 2D Gaussian mixture example under a variety of experiments. However, the limited experimental validation represents a significant weakness. The lack of testing on complex datasets, such as images, significantly diminishes the assessment of the actual impact of the work. Although, the method itself demonstrates high potential, a high impact can not be reasonably deduced at the current time.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving Alignment in LVLMs with Debiased Self-Judgment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20655v1)
- **Authors**: Sihan Yang, Chenhang Cui, Zihao Zhao, Yiyang Zhou, Weilong Yan, Ying Wei, Huaxiu Yao
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) and Large Visual-Language Models (LVLMs) have opened up new opportunities for integrating visual and linguistic modalities. However, effectively aligning these modalities remains challenging, often leading to hallucinations--where generated outputs are not grounded in the visual input--and raising safety concerns across various domains. Existing alignment methods, such as instruction tuning and preference tuning, often rely on external datasets, human annotations, or complex post-processing, which limit scalability and increase costs. To address these challenges, we propose a novel approach that generates the debiased self-judgment score, a self-evaluation metric created internally by the model without relying on external resources. This enables the model to autonomously improve alignment. Our method enhances both decoding strategies and preference tuning processes, resulting in reduced hallucinations, enhanced safety, and improved overall capability. Empirical results show that our approach significantly outperforms traditional methods, offering a more effective solution for aligning LVLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Improving Alignment in LVLMs with Debiased Self-Judgment":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the alignment problem in Large Visual-Language Models (LVLMs), focusing on reducing hallucinations and enhancing safety. The core idea is to leverage the model's *intrinsic* confidence in its own outputs as a self-judgment score, which can then be used to guide decoding and preference tuning. However, the authors acknowledge that this self-judgment is biased towards the textual modality. To address this, they propose a "debiasing" method that removes the influence of textual priors from the self-judgment score.  This debiased score is then used in three applications: (1) Debiased Self-Guided Decoding (DSGD) to reduce hallucinations during inference, (2) Fine-Grained Self-Defense (FGSD) to mitigate unsafe content, and (3) Debiased Self-Rewarding (DSR) to improve the model's overall capabilities through preference fine-tuning.  The authors present empirical results demonstrating the effectiveness of their approach across various benchmarks, claiming state-of-the-art performance in hallucination mitigation and safety, as well as significant improvements in overall capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using a model's internal confidence as a self-judgment score has been explored in LLMs. The primary novelty lies in the *debiasing* method specifically tailored for LVLMs, aiming to correct the text-modality bias. While potentially useful, the debiasing technique itself (subtracting a text-only self-judgment score) is relatively straightforward. The combination of this debiasing with sentence level preference tuning is also not new and it exists in other works (e.g., see Volcano paper). The significant contributions from the paper is more on the experiments and less on the algorithm design. The main algorithm is relatively simple but they demonstrated it can achieve significant improvement in practical application.
*   **Significance:**  The alignment problem in LVLMs is a crucial area of research, and hallucinations and safety concerns hinder real-world deployment. If the proposed approach demonstrably reduces these issues without relying on expensive external resources (human annotations or powerful models), it represents a valuable contribution. The authors claim strong performance gains. They have provided extensive set of experiments to show their design has the potential of becoming practical and scalable solution. However, The reliance on access to token logits restricts the applicability of the method to open-source models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Practical Approach:**  The method aims to be self-contained, reducing reliance on external resources, which is crucial for scalability and real-world applicability.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents results across a wide range of benchmarks, covering hallucination, safety, and general capabilities, using multiple LVLMs.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of text-modality bias in self-judgment.
    *   **Strong Results:** The experimental results indicate substantial improvements in the tested areas.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty:** The core "debiasing" technique, while potentially effective, is conceptually simple and may not be a significant algorithmic leap. It's more of a practical application of a known concept.
    *   **Dependence on Logit Access:** A major limitation is the requirement to access the model's token logits. This limits the applicability of the method to open-source or API-accessible models where these probabilities are exposed. The authors acknowledged this limitation.
    *   **Simplistic Debiasing:** The debiasing approach only mitigates text modality bias by the subtraction method. It is not clear if this can fully address the intrinsic safety issues. More fine-grained modeling or sophisticated methods can potentially lead to better performance.
    *   **Limited Generalizability Claims:** While the paper shows a few examples to support that the proposed solution has better generalizability, there is not clear theoretical justifications. More discussions and experiments can be provided.
    *  **Lack of comparison of performance to models after standard alignment process:** The paper demonstrates the model is better compared to the starting pre-trained version of the LVLM. However, there is no direct comparison with the best performing and already aligned LVLM. This might overestimate the effectiveness of the model to improve the alignment.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research by highlighting the importance of addressing modality biases in self-judgment methods for LVLMs. It also provides a practical and relatively simple approach that other researchers can build upon. The focus on reducing reliance on external resources is also a valuable direction for the field.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the existing body of work on self-judgment in LLMs, the somewhat limited novelty of the debiasing technique, and the restrictions related to logit access, a score of 6 is appropriate. While the empirical results are compelling and demonstrate the potential of the approach, the constraints on applicability and the incremental nature of the algorithmic contribution prevent a higher score. It's a solid, well-executed piece of engineering that addresses a practical problem, but lacks a truly transformative innovation. The strengths primarily stem from the thorough experimentation and practical focus.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### CodecBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Acoustic and Semantic Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20660v1)
- **Authors**: Ruifan Deng, Yitian Gong, Qinghui Gao, Luozhijie Jin, Qinyuan Cheng, Zhaoye Fei, Shimin Li, Xipeng Qiu
- **Abstract**: With the rise of multimodal large language models (LLMs), audio codec plays an increasingly vital role in encoding audio into discrete tokens, enabling integration of audio into text-based LLMs. Current audio codec captures two types of information: acoustic and semantic. As audio codec is applied to diverse scenarios in speech language model , it needs to model increasingly complex information and adapt to varied contexts, such as scenarios with multiple speakers, background noise, or richer paralinguistic information. However, existing codec's own evaluation has been limited by simplistic metrics and scenarios, and existing benchmarks for audio codec are not designed for complex application scenarios, which limits the assessment performance on complex datasets for acoustic and semantic capabilities. We introduce CodecBench, a comprehensive evaluation dataset to assess audio codec performance from both acoustic and semantic perspectives across four data domains. Through this benchmark, we aim to identify current limitations, highlight future research directions, and foster advances in the development of audio codec. The codes are available at https://github.com/RayYuki/CodecBench.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces CodecBench, a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating audio codecs from both acoustic and semantic perspectives across four data domains (speech, music, sound, and general audio). The motivation stems from the increasing importance of audio codecs in multimodal large language models (LLMs) and the limitations of existing evaluation methods, which often rely on simplistic metrics and datasets that don't fully capture the complexities of real-world scenarios. CodecBench addresses this by providing a rich dataset, diverse evaluation metrics (including acoustic fidelity and semantic alignment measures like ASR probing and classification tasks), and in-depth analysis of several recent codec models. The goal is to identify current limitations, highlight future research directions, and foster advances in audio codec development for multimodal applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

While the paper addresses a relevant and timely problem, its novelty and significance are somewhat limited. The idea of benchmarking audio codecs for LLM applications is not entirely new, as evidenced by the mention of Codec-SUPERB. CodecBench's main contributions lie in:

1.  **Dataset Composition:** The curation of a diverse dataset is a definite strength, especially the inclusion of a self-collected dataset and the emphasis on complex, real-world audio scenarios. However, the sheer size of each category might not be significantly larger than that of existing benchmarks.  A more detailed analysis and comparison of the dataset in terms of the size, noise, diversity, and semantic complexity with existing benchmarks would significantly improve the paper's strength.
2.  **Metric Suite:** The inclusion of ASR probing and classification tasks for semantic evaluation is a valuable addition beyond traditional acoustic metrics. However, these metrics, while useful, aren't fundamentally novel methodologies. The ARCH inspired classification task has not been described in significant depth, leaving questions about its specific formulation and advantages.  More justifications need to be made to state the superiority of the proposed metric suite.
3.  **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides an extensive evaluation of several recent codec models, which is beneficial for understanding their strengths and weaknesses. However, the analysis relies primarily on established metrics and doesn't offer groundbreaking insights into codec performance beyond what could be expected. There needs to be clear justifications and insights.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Methodological Novelty:** The core contribution of the paper is centered around creating a more comprehensive benchmark with carefully chosen metrics and datasets rather than introducing fundamentally novel techniques for evaluation or codec design. The techniques and methods used for analysis are all existing.

*   **Lack of Specificity in Analysis:** The conclusions drawn from the experimental results are sometimes too general. For example, simply stating that DAC performs well at high bitrates or that BigCodec performs well in single-codebook settings lacks depth. The *why* behind these observations should be discussed, connecting the architectures or training methods of these codecs to their performance on specific metrics. What is the trade off between higher bit rate and single-codebook settings and how does it related to other aspects in LLMs?

*   **Insufficient Comparison to Existing Benchmarks:** While the paper mentions Codec-SUPERB, it doesn't thoroughly compare CodecBench's advantages beyond dataset composition and the inclusion of semantic metrics. A more rigorous comparison of how CodecBench addresses the shortcomings of existing benchmarks and provides added value is necessary. What scenarios and evaluation techniques does CodecBench allow that other benchmarks cannot?

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies primarily in providing a more comprehensive and realistic evaluation framework for audio codecs that caters to the growing needs of speech language models. It provides a valuable resource for the community to test and improve future codec designs. However, the impact will be limited by the lack of fundamental methodological advances. The CodecBench provides a comprehensive way to benchmark and evaluate codecs. The benchmark itself does not make the advances, but does make the evaluation of future models more thorough.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the lack of significant methodological novelty and the somewhat limited depth of analysis, while acknowledging the value of the curated dataset and the inclusion of semantic metrics, a score of 6 is appropriate. The benchmark provides some improvements on current codec evaluation and does well in filling some current gaps.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 6/10

### Amadeus: Autoregressive Model with Bidirectional Attribute Modelling for Symbolic Music
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20665v1)
- **Authors**: Hongju Su, Ke Li, Lan Yang, Honggang Zhang, Yi-Zhe Song
- **Abstract**: Existing state-of-the-art symbolic music generation models predominantly adopt autoregressive or hierarchical autoregressive architectures, modelling symbolic music as a sequence of attribute tokens with unidirectional temporal dependencies, under the assumption of a fixed, strict dependency structure among these attributes. However, we observe that using different attributes as the initial token in these models leads to comparable performance. This suggests that the attributes of a musical note are, in essence, a concurrent and unordered set, rather than a temporally dependent sequence. Based on this insight, we introduce Amadeus, a novel symbolic music generation framework. Amadeus adopts a two-level architecture: an autoregressive model for note sequences and a bidirectional discrete diffusion model for attributes. To enhance performance, we propose Music Latent Space Discriminability Enhancement Strategy(MLSDES), incorporating contrastive learning constraints that amplify discriminability of intermediate music representations. The Conditional Information Enhancement Module (CIEM) simultaneously strengthens note latent vector representation via attention mechanisms, enabling more precise note decoding. We conduct extensive experiments on unconditional and text-conditioned generation tasks. Amadeus significantly outperforms SOTA models across multiple metrics while achieving at least 4$\times$ speed-up. Furthermore, we demonstrate training-free, fine-grained note attribute control feasibility using our model. To explore the upper performance bound of the Amadeus architecture, we compile the largest open-source symbolic music dataset to date, AMD (Amadeus MIDI Dataset), supporting both pre-training and fine-tuning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Amadeus paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Amadeus, a new symbolic music generation framework.  It departs from standard autoregressive models by employing a two-level architecture.  First, an autoregressive model generates note sequences. Second, a bidirectional discrete diffusion model decodes these note-level latent variables into multi-dimensional attributes (pitch, duration, etc.). To improve performance, the authors propose a Music Latent Space Discriminability Enhancement Strategy (MLSDES) based on contrastive learning and a Conditional Information Enhancement Module (CIEM) that uses attention mechanisms to refine note representations. The authors present experiments demonstrating Amadeus's superiority over existing state-of-the-art methods in generation quality, control, and speed, across both unconditional and text-conditioned music generation tasks. Finally, the authors release a large-scale symbolic music dataset (AMD) for pre-training and fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its hybrid architecture.  Decoupling note-level generation from attribute decoding using a bidirectional diffusion model is a significant departure from existing approaches that primarily rely on unidirectional autoregressive models or hierarchical autoregressive architectures. The MLSDES and CIEM components further refine this architecture. The proposed approach effectively addresses the limitations of existing methods by modeling intra-note attributes as a concurrent and unordered set, rather than a temporally dependent sequence. The creation and release of the AMD dataset is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The Amadeus framework offers several significant advantages. The bidirectional diffusion model at the attribute level enables more efficient parallel decoding and allows for greater control over individual attributes. The experiments demonstrate Amadeus's superior generation quality, faster inference speeds, and controllable generation capabilities. The release of the AMD dataset should foster further research in symbolic music generation by enabling larger-scale pre-training and fine-tuning efforts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Hybrid Architecture:** The core architecture is well-motivated and elegantly addresses the limitations of existing autoregressive approaches.
    *   **Component Design:**  The MLSDES and CIEM modules are specifically designed to improve the representation and decoding process.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The authors conduct thorough experiments on multiple tasks, demonstrating superior performance across various metrics.
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The release of the AMD dataset is a valuable community resource.
    *   **Speed:** Achieved at least a 4x speed up in generation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The two-level architecture with diffusion models increases the complexity of the framework, potentially making it more challenging to train and optimize than simpler autoregressive models.
    *   **Limited Generalization:** While the paper provides a comprehensive evaluation, the experiments are primarily focused on Western musical styles. Further research is needed to assess the framework's generalizability to other musical traditions.
    *   **Limited analysis of limitations**: The limitations section focuses on the impact of the training dataset and does not provide a comprehensive theoretical evaluation of potential pitfalls when generating music outside the domain of Western styles or even sub-styles of Western Music.

*   **Impact:** This work has the potential to significantly influence future research in symbolic music generation. Its innovative architecture and the release of a large-scale dataset should stimulate new research directions and enable advancements in the field. Specifically, its method to parallelize attribute generation through diffusion models is a breakthrough.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I assign the paper a score of 8. The decoupling of sequence and attribute generation, use of diffusion models, and overall architecture make a tangible contribution. The release of the AMD is also notable. The score is not higher due to potential complexities in training diffusion models and a need to better evaluate performance beyond the tested datasets.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 8/10

### Leveraging Large Language Models for Generating Research Topic Ontologies: A Multi-Disciplinary Study
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20693v1)
- **Authors**: Tanay Aggarwal, Angelo Salatino, Francesco Osborne, Enrico Motta
- **Abstract**: Ontologies and taxonomies of research fields are critical for managing and organising scientific knowledge, as they facilitate efficient classification, dissemination and retrieval of information. However, the creation and maintenance of such ontologies are expensive and time-consuming tasks, usually requiring the coordinated effort of multiple domain experts. Consequently, ontologies in this space often exhibit uneven coverage across different disciplines, limited inter-domain connectivity, and infrequent updating cycles. In this study, we investigate the capability of several large language models to identify semantic relationships among research topics within three academic domains: biomedicine, physics, and engineering. The models were evaluated under three distinct conditions: zero-shot prompting, chain-of-thought prompting, and fine-tuning on existing ontologies. Additionally, we assessed the cross-domain transferability of fine-tuned models by measuring their performance when trained in one domain and subsequently applied to a different one. To support this analysis, we introduce PEM-Rel-8K, a novel dataset consisting of over 8,000 relationships extracted from the most widely adopted taxonomies in the three disciplines considered in this study: MeSH, PhySH, and IEEE. Our experiments demonstrate that fine-tuning LLMs on PEM-Rel-8K yields excellent performance across all disciplines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Leveraging Large Language Models for Generating Research Topic Ontologies: A Multi-Disciplinary Study" investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for automatically generating research topic ontologies.  It focuses on identifying semantic relationships (broader, narrower, same-as, other) between research topics across three distinct scientific domains: Biomedicine (MeSH), Physics (PhySH), and Engineering (IEEE). The study evaluates various LLMs under different conditions: zero-shot prompting, chain-of-thought prompting, and fine-tuning.  A key contribution is the introduction of PEM-Rel-8K, a novel multi-disciplinary dataset containing over 8,000 relationships extracted from the three domain-specific ontologies.  The paper explores the cross-domain transferability of fine-tuned models and demonstrates that fine-tuning LLMs on PEM-Rel-8K leads to excellent performance across all disciplines, often surpassing zero-shot and CoT approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant contribution by creating and releasing PEM-Rel-8K, a valuable resource for the research community.  While LLMs have been used for ontology generation before, the focus on multi-disciplinary research topics and the exploration of cross-domain transferability using a purpose-built dataset adds considerable novelty. The investigation of several recent open-weight LLMs is also a strong aspect.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem: the difficulty and expense of manually creating and maintaining research topic ontologies.  Automating this process using LLMs has the potential to improve the classification, dissemination, and retrieval of scientific knowledge. The results demonstrate the feasibility of using LLMs, particularly with fine-tuning, to generate useful ontologies.  The cross-domain transferability findings are significant, suggesting that a single, well-trained model can be applied across multiple disciplines, reducing the need for domain-specific expertise.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** PEM-Rel-8K is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a comprehensive evaluation of multiple LLMs under various conditions.
    *   **Cross-Domain Transferability Analysis:**  The investigation of cross-domain transferability is insightful and practically relevant.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The paper emphasizes reproducibility and provides access to the code and dataset.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** While three disciplines are covered, extending the analysis to a wider range of scientific domains (e.g., Social Sciences, Humanities) would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Limited Semantic Relations:** The study focuses on only four semantic relationships. Exploring more complex relations (e.g., "causes," "is part of") would make the generated ontologies more expressive.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** Focus mainly on classification accuracy.  Evaluating the *quality* of the generated ontologies from a knowledge representation point of view, e.g., by measuring inconsistencies would add robustness.
    *   **Error Analysis:** While confusion matrices are provided, a more detailed qualitative analysis of the common error patterns and their implications for ontology quality would be valuable. Exploring the quality of the "other" examples as negative examples would be helpful.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the field of scholarly knowledge management by providing a practical approach for automated ontology generation. It can inspire future research in:
    *   Developing more sophisticated LLM-based methods for ontology creation.
    *   Exploring the use of LLMs for automatically expanding and integrating existing ontologies.
    *   Creating AI-powered tools that leverage these automatically generated ontologies to improve research discovery and collaboration.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field through the PEM-Rel-8K dataset and a thorough evaluation of LLMs for research topic ontology generation. The focus on cross-domain transferability is a key strength, demonstrating the practical applicability of the approach. However, the limited scope (number of disciplines and semantic relations) and the lack of deeper qualitative error analysis prevent a higher score. Nevertheless, the paper makes a strong case for the potential of LLMs in automating the creation of research ontologies and is likely to stimulate further research in this area. A higher score would be justified with more extensive validation across a broader set of domains and ontology quality metrics.

- **Classification**: cs.DL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Token Buncher: Shielding LLMs from Harmful Reinforcement Learning Fine-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20697v1)
- **Authors**: Weitao Feng, Lixu Wang, Tianyi Wei, Jie Zhang, Chongyang Gao, Sinong Zhan, Peizhuo Lv, Wei Dong
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) continue to grow in capability, so do the risks of harmful misuse through fine-tuning. While most prior studies assume that attackers rely on supervised fine-tuning (SFT) for such misuse, we systematically demonstrate that reinforcement learning (RL) enables adversaries to more effectively break safety alignment and facilitate advanced harmful task assistance, under matched computational budgets. To counter this emerging threat, we propose TokenBuncher, the first effective defense specifically targeting RL-based harmful fine-tuning. TokenBuncher suppresses the foundation on which RL relies: model response uncertainty. By constraining uncertainty, RL-based fine-tuning can no longer exploit distinct reward signals to drive the model toward harmful behaviors. We realize this defense through entropy-as-reward RL and a Token Noiser mechanism designed to prevent the escalation of expert-domain harmful capabilities. Extensive experiments across multiple models and RL algorithms show that TokenBuncher robustly mitigates harmful RL fine-tuning while preserving benign task utility and finetunability. Our results highlight that RL-based harmful fine-tuning poses a greater systemic risk than SFT, and that TokenBuncher provides an effective and general defense.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "TOKEN BUNCHER: Shielding LLMs from Harmful Reinforcement Learning Fine-Tuning" addresses the emerging threat of Reinforcement Learning (RL) based harmful fine-tuning of Large Language Models (LLMs).  It systematically demonstrates that RL enables adversaries to more effectively break safety alignment compared to supervised fine-tuning (SFT), even under matched computational budgets. The paper introduces TOKENBUNCHER, a novel defense mechanism that constrains model response uncertainty, a key ingredient for RL-based exploitation. TOKENBUNCHER achieves this through an entropy-as-reward RL scheme and a Token Noiser mechanism to prevent escalation of expert-domain harmful capabilities. Experimental results demonstrate that TOKENBUNCHER robustly mitigates harmful RL fine-tuning while preserving utility and finetunability.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper identifies a crucial vulnerability – the increased effectiveness of RL in circumventing safety measures in LLMs compared to SFT.  Prior work has largely focused on SFT attacks, making the RL-based attack vector a novel and significant contribution. TOKENBUNCHER itself is a novel defense strategy specifically designed to counter RL-based harmful fine-tuning by directly targeting response uncertainty.
*   **Significance:**  The paper highlights a potentially underestimated systemic risk: as open-source models approach frontier-level performance and fine-tuning-as-a-service becomes widespread, RL-based attacks pose a severe threat to LLM safety.  If not addressed, this could lead to widespread misuse and harmful applications of LLMs. The significance stems from the demonstration that *existing* defenses against SFT are inadequate against RL-based attacks, and the introduction of TOKENBUNCHER as a defense strategy.  The paper also provides valuable insights into the differences between SFT and RL from an adversarial perspective, informing future research.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough comparison between SFT and RL-based attacks, identifying the advantages of RL.
    *   **Effective Defense:** TOKENBUNCHER demonstrates strong performance across multiple models and RL algorithms, significantly reducing harmfulness compared to baseline defenses.
    *   **Preservation of Utility:** The defense maintains benign task utility and finetunability, a crucial aspect for practical deployment.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The threat model and problem formulation are well-defined, making the paper easy to understand and build upon.
    *   **Implementation Detail and Reproducibility:** The paper includes sufficient implementation details (although the provided github will need to be checked), enhancing reproducibility.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Scalability and Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't extensively discuss the computational overhead of training with TOKENBUNCHER. Real-world deployment would necessitate an evaluation of the resource requirements.
    *   **Specific RL Algorithms:** While TOKENBUNCHER is evaluated against several RL algorithms, it's not an exhaustive coverage of the RL landscape. Future work should investigate its performance against a wider range of more modern or specialized algorithms.
    *   **Reward Model Reliance:** While the authors state that TOKENBUNCHER does not require a reward model, the experiments use a reward model to gauge effectiveness of a fine-tuning. Furthermore, a reward model is necessary to generate the entropy, making it unclear how truly separate these two concepts are.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings are likely to influence future research in LLM security and defense.  It calls for a shift in focus towards RL-based vulnerabilities and provides a concrete defensive strategy to mitigate this risk. The TOKENBUNCHER defense could serve as a starting point for developing more robust and generalizable defenses against RL-based attacks. Furthermore, the paper makes clear the potential of the exploitation of high-risk knowledge data, an important point that future works should consider.
*   **Missing points:** There is little discussion on limitations for the TOKENBUNCHER defense. What type of algorithms are the authors most concerned about? How does the noise change with increased computational resources? It would also be helpful to see a more detailed discussion in the appendix.
*   **Other Considerations:** The paper acknowledges the potential for adaptive attacks and proposes a countermeasure. However, a more in-depth analysis of adaptive attack strategies and the robustness of TOKENBUNCHER against them is warranted.
    *   Overall, the paper presents a compelling and timely contribution to the field of LLM security. The identification of RL-based vulnerabilities and the introduction of TOKENBUNCHER provide valuable insights and a practical defense strategy. While the paper has some limitations, its novelty and potential influence justify a strong rating.
    *   *Note: This evaluation assumed the github provides a reproduction and proper implementation.*

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper makes a significant contribution by highlighting the threat of RL-based harmful fine-tuning and introducing a novel and effective defense. The experimental results are strong, and the potential impact on the field is substantial. The limitations regarding scalability, the coverage of RL algorithms, lack of details and potential overconfidence due to reliance on a reward model are the main factors preventing a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### EEGDM: Learning EEG Representation with Latent Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20705v1)
- **Authors**: Shaocong Wang, Tong Liu, Ming Li, Minjing Yu, Yong-Jin Liu
- **Abstract**: While electroencephalography (EEG) signal analysis using deep learning has shown great promise, existing approaches still face significant challenges in learning generalizable representations that perform well across diverse tasks, particularly when training data is limited. Current EEG representation learning methods including EEGPT and LaBraM typically rely on simple masked reconstruction objective, which may not fully capture the rich semantic information and complex patterns inherent in EEG signals. In this paper, we propose EEGDM, a novel self-supervised EEG representation learning method based on the latent diffusion model, which leverages EEG signal generation as a self-supervised objective, turning the diffusion model into a strong representation learner capable of capturing EEG semantics. EEGDM incorporates an EEG encoder that distills EEG signals and their channel augmentations into a compact representation, acting as conditional information to guide the diffusion model for generating EEG signals. This design endows EEGDM with a compact latent space, which not only offers ample control over the generative process but also can be leveraged for downstream tasks. Experimental results show that EEGDM (1) can reconstruct high-quality EEG signals, (2) effectively learns robust representations, and (3) achieves competitive performance with modest pre-training data size across diverse downstream tasks, underscoring its generalizability and practical utility.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EEGDM, a novel self-supervised framework for learning EEG representations using a latent diffusion model.  Unlike existing methods that rely on masked reconstruction, EEGDM leverages EEG signal generation as a self-supervised objective, turning the diffusion model into a strong representation learner. The method incorporates an EEG encoder to distill EEG signals and their channel augmentations into a compact latent representation, conditioning the diffusion process.  The latent space derived from PCA ensures an improved SNR. Experiments demonstrate that EEGDM can reconstruct high-quality EEG signals, learn robust representations, and achieve competitive performance on diverse downstream tasks with modest pre-training data. The authors highlight EEGDM's generalizability and practical utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in adapting latent diffusion models (LDMs) for EEG *representation* learning, rather than just generation, and conditioning the LDM on EEG signals. This is a meaningful departure from existing EEG representation learning methods based on masked autoencoders or contrastive learning. The use of PCA for denoising and improving SNR before feeding into the LDM is also a useful and practical addition. Channel augmentation for enriching conditional information is a commonly seen trick, but the paper effectively leverages it. The combination of these techniques, along with a DiT-based denoiser, contribute to a novel architecture specifically designed for the challenges of EEG data.

*   **Significance:** EEG data is notoriously noisy and variable, making robust representation learning a critical challenge. The paper's approach, leveraging the generative capabilities of diffusion models, has the potential to address these issues. The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of EEGDM across diverse downstream tasks, indicating its potential for broader applicability in EEG analysis. The authors address the low SNR present in EEG signals by performing PCA, a valuable pre-processing step that enhances representation learning in downstream tasks. Moreover, it demonstrates strong performance even with smaller datasets when compared to larger models like LaBraM, showcasing practical utility in resource-constrained settings. Demonstrating strong performance with limited data would be highly impactful given the difficulty in collecting large EEG datasets, which can be further expanded to other similar tasks such as EMG.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel approach:** Adapting latent diffusion models for EEG *representation* learning is a unique direction.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** Demonstrated competitive or superior performance on multiple downstream tasks.
    *   **SNR Consideration:** Effectively addresses the inherent noise in EEG signals using PCA.
    *   **Generalizability:**  Demonstrates effectiveness across diverse tasks (abnormality detection, event classification, motor imagery).
    *   **Data efficiency:** Achieving competitive results with smaller pre-training datasets is valuable.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental improvements:** While the core idea is novel, the individual components (PCA, channel augmentation, DiT architecture) are well-established.  The improvement comes from the *combination* and careful tuning.
    *   **Limited theoretical analysis:**  The paper primarily focuses on empirical results, with less in-depth theoretical analysis of why the diffusion model is particularly well-suited for EEG representation learning compared to other generative models.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study demonstrates the usefulness of certain augmentations and the PCA, a more comprehensive component ablation study could also be highly beneficial.
    *   **Computational cost:** Diffusion models can be computationally expensive to train and sample from, potentially limiting their widespread adoption. While the paper highlights data efficiency, the training cost could still be a barrier.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has a good chance of influencing the field. It opens up a new avenue for EEG representation learning using diffusion models.  If future research builds upon this work and addresses the computational cost limitations, it could become a standard approach. The pre-trained EEGDM model itself could become a valuable resource for the EEG research community.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

EEGDM presents a novel and empirically sound approach to EEG representation learning by leveraging diffusion models. The paper demonstrably achieves competitive results across multiple downstream tasks, and shows generalizability and efficacy. However, the use of diffusion models for the *generative process* in itself isn't new, and rather the novelty is in their adaptation for representation learning, making it incremental. The lack of in-depth theoretical analysis and the potential computational expense also weigh against a higher score. Given the potential practical impact and novel adaptation, it merits a 7.5.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Addressing Tokenization Inconsistency in Steganography and Watermarking Based on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20718v1)
- **Authors**: Ruiyi Yan, Yugo Murawaki
- **Abstract**: Large language models have significantly enhanced the capacities and efficiency of text generation. On the one hand, they have improved the quality of text-based steganography. On the other hand, they have also underscored the importance of watermarking as a safeguard against malicious misuse. In this study, we focus on tokenization inconsistency (TI) between Alice and Bob in steganography and watermarking, where TI can undermine robustness. Our investigation reveals that the problematic tokens responsible for TI exhibit two key characteristics: infrequency and temporariness. Based on these findings, we propose two tailored solutions for TI elimination: a stepwise verification method for steganography and a post-hoc rollback method for watermarking. Experiments show that (1) compared to traditional disambiguation methods in steganography, directly addressing TI leads to improvements in fluency, imperceptibility, and anti-steganalysis capacity; (2) for watermarking, addressing TI enhances detectability and robustness against attacks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of tokenization inconsistency (TI) on the robustness of steganography and watermarking techniques using large language models (LLMs). It identifies two key characteristics of tokens responsible for TI: infrequency and temporariness. Based on these findings, the authors propose two tailored solutions: a stepwise verification method for steganography and a post-hoc rollback method for watermarking. Experimental results demonstrate that addressing TI directly improves fluency, imperceptibility, and anti-steganalysis capacity in steganography, and enhances detectability and robustness in watermarking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its focused investigation of tokenization inconsistency within the context of LLM-based steganography and watermarking. While TI itself is not a completely new concept, its systematic analysis specifically concerning the detokenization-retokenization pipeline in these applications, and the characterization of inconsistent tokens as infrequent and temporary, are valuable contributions. The proposed methods for addressing TI, particularly the stepwise verification for steganography, offer a practical approach for improving robustness compared to existing disambiguation techniques. However, the rollback method for watermarking seems less novel, resembling established methods.

*   **Significance:** The findings have implications for the design and implementation of more reliable and robust steganography and watermarking systems using LLMs. The study highlights a previously overlooked vulnerability that could be exploited to undermine the effectiveness of these techniques.
